**8.34.2**  Then from the flank and from the wing, and with the wings from the right as well, the Kurus with weapons raised rushed upon Bhīma in battle.

**8.34.3**  Karṇa too, seeing the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra fleeing with averted faces, drove his swan-white, peerless steeds to where Vṛkodara was.

**8.34.4**  Urged on, O great king, by Śalya who shone in battle, those horses, reaching Bhīmasena's chariot, were brought to a halt.

**8.34.5**  Seeing Karṇa drawing near, Bhīma, filled with wrath, set his mind upon Karṇa's destruction, O bull of the Bhāratas.

**8.34.6**  He said to the hero Sātyaki and to Dhṛṣṭadyumna, son of Pṛṣata: 'Guard this king, the righteous-souled Yudhiṣṭhira, who, before my very eyes, has just barely escaped from a great peril.'

**8.34.7**  'Before me the king was stripped of all his equipage, to please Duryodhana, by the evil-souled son of Rādhā.'

**8.34.8**  'This day, O son of Pṛṣata, I shall make an end of that sorrow. Either I shall slay Karṇa in battle, or he shall slay me in a most terrible fight—this I tell you truly.'

**8.34.9**  'This day I give over the king into your keeping as a sacred trust; do you all, without anxiety, strive together for his protection.'

**8.34.10**  Having spoken thus, the mighty-armed one advanced against the son of Adhiratha, making all the quarters resound with a great lion's roar.

**8.34.11**  Seeing Bhīma, who delighted in battle, swiftly approaching, the lord and sovereign of the Madras spoke thus to the son of the charioteer:

**8.34.12**  'Behold, Karṇa, the mighty-armed delight of the Pāṇḍus, enraged and surely eager to pour out upon you a wrath long stored up.'

**8.34.13**  'Never before have I seen such a form upon him, O Karṇa—not when Abhimanyu was slain, nor when the rākṣasa Ghaṭotkaca fell.'

**8.34.14**  'In his wrath, fit to hold back the whole of the three worlds, he wears a form fair yet terrible, like the fire of the world's end.'

**8.34.15**  As the lord of the Madras spoke thus to the son of Rādhā, O king, Vṛkodara, ablaze with anger, bore down upon Karṇa.

**8.34.16**  Beholding Bhīma, who rejoiced in battle, come on in that fashion, the son of Rādhā, smiling as it were, spoke these words to Śalya:

**8.34.17**  'The words you have spoken to me this day concerning Bhīmasena, O lord of the Madra people, O mighty one, are true—of this there is no doubt.'

**8.34.18**  'This Vṛkodara is brave and heroic and wrathful, heedless of his own body and of his very life, and surpassing in strength.'

**8.34.19**  'When he dwelt in concealment in the city of Virāṭa, to do a kindness to Draupadī, relying on the strength of his arms alone, keeping his identity hidden, he slew Kīcaka together with his kinsmen.'

**8.34.20**  'This day, at the head of the battle, armed and frenzied with rage, he would march into the fray even against Death himself with uplifted, ready rod, the servant of Yama.'

**8.34.21**  'But this is my heart's desire, long cherished: that I should slay Arjuna in battle, or that Dhanaṃjaya should slay me. Through this meeting with Bhīma, that wish of mine may perhaps be fulfilled this very day.'

**8.34.22**  'For if Bhīmasena be slain, or be deprived of his chariot, then Pārtha will come against me—and that will be well for me. Resolve quickly upon what you deem fit here.'

**8.34.23**  Hearing these words of the great-souled son of Rādhā, Śalya spoke thus to the son of the charioteer as he came on:

**8.34.24**  'Advance, O mighty-armed one, against the mighty Bhīmasena; and when you have driven Bhīmasena off, then you shall meet Phalguna.'

**8.34.25**  'That desire which you have cherished in your heart since long ago shall surely be fulfilled, O Karṇa—this I tell you truly.'

**8.34.26**  Thus addressed, Karṇa spoke again to Śalya: 'I shall slay Arjuna in the encounter, or Dhanaṃjaya shall slay me. Fix your mind upon the battle—drive on, drive on!' he urged.

**8.34.27**  Then Śalya swiftly drove the chariot, O lord of the people, to where Bhīma, the great archer, was putting the host to rout.

**8.34.28**  Then the blare of trumpets and the great clamor of kettledrums arose, O lord of kings, at the meeting of Karṇa and Bhīma.

**8.34.29**  Then the mighty Bhīmasena, filled with wrath, with bright sharp iron shafts drove your hard-to-assail army fleeing in all directions.

**8.34.30**  That clash was tumultuous and terrible in aspect, O lord of the people, fierce indeed, in the battle between Karṇa and the Pāṇḍava; and after a moment, O lord of kings, the Pāṇḍava charged upon Karṇa.

**8.34.31**  Seeing him charge on, Karṇa Vaikartana, the bull among men, struck him in wrath upon the chest, between the breasts, with an iron shaft; then that one of measureless soul overwhelmed him with showers of arrows.

**8.34.32**  Pierced by the son of the charioteer, he covered Karṇa over with feathered shafts and struck him with nine sharp, smooth-jointed arrows.

**8.34.33**  Karṇa cut his bow in two at the middle with a feathered shaft; then, when his bow was severed, he struck him between the breasts with a most sharp iron shaft that pierced through all armor.

**8.34.34**  Taking up another bow, Vṛkodara, O king, who knew the vital points, pierced the son of the charioteer in his vitals with keenly whetted shafts and roared a mighty roar, as if to shake heaven and earth.

**8.34.35**  Karṇa tormented him with five-and-twenty iron shafts, as one might torment with firebrands a rut-maddened, haughty elephant in the forest.

**8.34.36**  Then the Pāṇḍava, his limbs torn by the arrows, frenzied with rage, his eyes coppery with fury and indignation, in his desire to slay the son of the charioteer—

**8.34.37**  —fitted to his bow an arrow of great speed, of supreme striking power, a shaft that could cleave even mountains.

**8.34.38**  Drawing the bow with might back to his ear, that surpassing son of the Wind-god, the great archer, in his wrath let the shaft fly, eager to slay Karṇa.

**8.34.39**  Loosed by the mighty one, that arrow, with the sound of thunderbolt and lightning, rent Karṇa in the battle as a thunderbolt's force cleaves a mountain.

**8.34.40**  Struck by Bhīmasena, the son of the charioteer, O perpetuator of the Kurus, that commander of armies sank down senseless upon the floor of his chariot.

**8.34.41**  Then the lord of the Madras, seeing the son of the charioteer fallen senseless, bore Karṇa, who shone in battle, away from the field upon his chariot.

**8.34.42**  Then, Karṇa being vanquished, Bhīmasena routed the great host of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son, as Indra routed the host of the Dānavas.

**8.35.1**  Dhṛtarāṣṭra said: A most difficult deed has been done by Bhīma, O Saṃjaya, in that the mighty-armed Karṇa was struck down upon the floor of his chariot.

**8.35.2**  'Karṇa alone in battle will slay the Sṛñjayas together with the Pāṇḍavas'—so, O charioteer, did Duryodhana tell me again and again.

**8.35.3**  Seeing the son of Rādhā vanquished by Bhīma in the encounter, what then did my son Duryodhana do thereafter?

**8.35.4**  Saṃjaya said: Seeing the son of Rādhā, the son of the charioteer, reeling in that great battle, the king with his vast army spoke to his own brothers:

**8.35.5**  'Go quickly—good fortune to you—and protect the son of Rādhā, who is sinking in the fathomless ocean of calamity that is the terror of Bhīmasena.'

**8.35.6**  Commanded thus by the king, eager to slay Bhīmasena, they advanced in fury like moths upon a flame.

**8.35.7**  Śrutāyus, Durdhara, Krātha, Vivitsu, Vikaṭa, Sama, Niṣaṅgin, Kavacin, Pāśin, and Nanda and Upananda—

**8.35.8**  —Duṣpradharṣa and Subāhu, Vātavega and Suvarcas, Dhanurgrāha and Durmada, and likewise Sattvasama and Saha—

**8.35.9**  —these valiant and mighty ones, surrounded by their chariots, having reached Bhīmasena, beset him on every side and loosed upon him from all directions volleys of arrows of many marks.

**8.35.10**  Sorely pressed by them, the mighty Bhīmasena swiftly slew, of those sons of yours rushing upon him, O lord of men, fifty chariot-warriors along with their fifty chariots.

**8.35.11**  Then, in his wrath, with a broad-headed shaft Bhīma struck off Vivitsu's head, decked with earrings and helmet and like the full moon; and so, O great king, slain by Bhīma, he fell to the earth.

**8.35.12**  Seeing that hero slain, his brothers from every side, O lord, charged in battle upon Bhīma of dreadful prowess.

**8.35.13**  Then with two other broad-headed shafts, Bhīma of dreadful prowess took the lives of two of your sons in that great battle.

**8.35.14**  Both fell to the earth like trees broken by the wind—Vikaṭa and Sama, the two of them, fair as the sons of gods, O king.

**8.35.15**  Then Bhīma in haste sent Krātha to the abode of Yama; struck by a most sharp iron shaft, he fell slain to the earth.

**8.35.16**  Then a piercing cry of woe arose, O lord of men, as your bow-bearing sons were slain, O king.

**8.35.17**  When their host was thrown into disorder, the mighty Bhīmasena dispatched Nanda and Upananda in battle to the dwelling of Yama.

**8.35.18**  Then your sons, stricken and dismayed, fled in terror, beholding Bhīmasena in the battle like Yama the all-ending destroyer at the world's close.

**8.35.19**  Seeing your sons slain, the high-minded son of the charioteer again drove his swan-white steeds to where the Pāṇḍava was.

**8.35.20**  Urged on by the king of the Madras, O great king, those horses, reaching Bhīmasena's chariot at speed, were brought to a stand.

**8.35.21**  That clash was tumultuous and terrible of aspect, O lord of the people, fierce indeed, O great king, in the battle between Karṇa and the Pāṇḍava.

**8.35.22**  Seeing those two great chariot-warriors joined in combat, O great king, the thought arose in me: how, indeed, will this turn out this day?

**8.35.23**  Then in a moment, O lord of kings, without much effort, smiling as it were, Karṇa made Bhīma of terrible deeds bereft of his chariot.

**8.35.24**  Bereft of his chariot, that best of the Bhāratas, laughing aloud, the mighty-armed one, peer of the wind, mace in hand, leapt down from his excellent car.

**8.35.25**  Seven hundred war-elephants, O king, with tusks like chariot-poles and trained to strike, Bhīma the scorcher of foes, his form fierce with wrath, struck down all at once.

**8.35.26**  Knowing the vital points, he smote them mightily, roaring as he struck — upon their gums, their eyes, their temples, their cheeks, and upon their tender spots.

**8.35.27**  Then they fled in terror; but, driven back again by their mahouts, they hemmed him in as clouds enshroud the sun.

**8.35.28**  Those seven hundred elephants, with their riders, weapons, and banners, he, standing on the ground, smote with his mace, as the wind scatters the autumn clouds.

**8.35.29**  Then the son of Kuntī crushed once more in battle fifty-two mighty elephants belonging to the son of Subala.

**8.35.30**  And the Pāṇḍava slew in the fight a full hundred chariots and hundreds of foot-soldiers besides, scorching your host.

**8.35.31**  Scorched by the sun and by the great-souled Bhīma, your army shrank together like a hide cast into fire.

**8.35.32**  Stricken with terror of Bhīma, O bull of the Bhāratas, your warriors forsook him in the battle and fled in all ten directions.

**8.35.33**  Five hundred other chariots, clattering, their warriors clad in shields and armor, rushed swiftly upon Bhīma with showers of arrows on every side.

**8.35.34**  All of them, with their charioteers, chariots, pennons, standards, and weapons, Bhīma crushed with his mace, as Viṣṇu crushed the Asuras.

**8.35.35**  Then three thousand horsemen, sent by Śakuni and esteemed as heroes, advanced against Bhīma, lances, spears, and javelins in their hands.

**8.35.36**  Going forth to meet those Yavana horsemen, the slayer of his finest foes, ranging along manifold paths, smote and crushed them to death.

**8.35.37**  Loud was the clamor of those stricken on every side, O Bhārata, as they were hewn down by his sword, like reeds being cut.

**8.35.38**  Thus having slain three thousand of the finest horses of the son of Subala, he mounted another chariot and in wrath advanced against the son of Rādhā.

**8.35.39**  Karṇa too in the battle, O king, covered with arrows the son of Dharma, that tamer of foes, and struck down his charioteer as well.

**8.35.40**  Then, seeing in the fight the chariot bolting away, the great car-warrior gave chase, scattering straight-flying arrows fledged with heron-feathers.

**8.35.41**  As he sped toward the king, the son of the Wind, in his wrath, veiling heaven and earth with arrows, covered him over with a net of shafts.

**8.35.42**  Then the son of Rādhā, that harrier of foes, swiftly wheeling back, covered Bhīma all about with whetted arrows.

**8.35.43**  As Karṇa was engrossed with Bhīmasena's chariot, O Bhārata, Sātyaki of measureless spirit assailed him, to take him in the flank; and Karṇa, though sorely afflicted by his shafts, turned upon him.

**8.35.44**  Closing with each other, those two bulls among all bowmen, those two high-minded ones, shone resplendent as they loosed their many-colored arrows.

**8.35.45**  From them both, O lord of kings, a dread net of arrows spread across the sky, terrible to behold, ruddy as the back of the krauñca, fierce in its aspect.

**8.35.46**  By the thousands of arrows loosed by those two we could no longer perceive the sun's radiance, nor the sky, nor the quarters, nor the intermediate quarters — let alone aught else.

**8.35.47**  The mighty rays of the noonday sun, blazing at midday, O king, were then all stolen away by those floods of arrows shot by Karṇa and Mādhava.

**8.35.48**  Seeing the son of Subala, Kṛtavarman, the son of Droṇa, the son of Adhiratha, and Kṛpa locked in conflict with the Pāṇḍavas, the Kurus turned back once more.

**8.35.49**  The din of their onset was fierce, O lord of peoples, terrifying as that of oceans roused to swelling by the rains.

**8.35.50**  Those two armies, deeply stirred at the sight of each other in the great battle, filled with mighty exultation, grappled with one another.

**8.35.51**  Then, when the sun had reached the midpoint of heaven, there began a battle the like of which had never before been seen nor heard of.

**8.35.52**  Flood of host meeting flood of host in the fray, rushed upon it with violence, as a torrent of waters rushes upon the sea.

**8.35.53**  A mighty roar arose from those floods of hosts as they thundered against one another, like the great clamor of the surging seas.

**8.35.54**  Those two armies, sweeping with speed each against the other, became merged into one, as two rivers blend at their confluence.

**8.35.55**  Then began a battle of dreadful aspect, O lord of peoples, between the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas, each coveting exceeding great glory.

**8.35.56**  As the Kurus roared there, their unbroken cries were heard, O king, of many kinds, calling out their names, O Bhārata.

**8.35.57**  Whatever distinction a man had in battle — by his father's line or his mother's, by his deeds or by his character — that he proclaimed aloud in the fight.

**8.35.58**  Beholding those heroes in the battle defying one another, my thought was, O king, that for these there was no life left.

**8.35.59**  And seeing the forms of those wrathful warriors of boundless energy, a keen fear came upon me — how will this come to pass?

**8.35.60**  Then those Pāṇḍavas, O king, and the great car-warriors of the Kauravas hewed at one another with sharp shafts, slaying each the other.

**8.36.1**  Saṃjaya said: Those kṣatriyas, O great king, each seeking the slaughter of the other, having become foes to one another, slew one another in the battle.

**8.36.2**  Floods of chariots and floods of horses and floods of men on every side, and floods of elephants, O great king, were locked together each with the other.

**8.36.3**  Of maces and iron clubs and of darting kaṇapas, of javelins, of bhiṇḍipālas, and of bhuśuṇḍīs on every side —

**8.36.4**  the collision we beheld in that exceedingly terrible battle; and showers of arrows fell on all sides like locusts.

**8.36.5**  Elephants closing with elephants struck one another down, and horses with horses in the battle, and car-warriors with car-warriors, foot-soldiers with bands of foot-soldiers, and horses with troops of horses.

**8.36.6**  Foot-soldiers crushed chariots and elephants, chariots crushed elephants and horses, and the swift-moving elephants in battle crushed the three arms, O king.

**8.36.7**  As the heroes fell there and cried out against one another, the battlefield grew terrible, like the slaughtering-place of beasts.

**8.36.8**  Strewn over with blood, the earth gleamed, O Bhārata, like the ground in the rains, thick-scattered with swarms of the indragopa beetles.

**8.36.9**  Or as a dusky young woman might wear two white garments dyed with deep crimson, so did the earth appear, dappled as it were with flesh and blood, and seeming as though wrought of gold.

**8.36.10**  Of severed heads and of arms together with thighs, of earrings flung about and of ornaments, O Bhārata —

**8.36.11**  of golden breast-plates upon their cords, and of the bodies of bowmen, and of armor with its banners — heaps of these fell there upon the ground.

**8.36.12**  Elephants closing with elephants tore them with the points of their tusks, and those elephants, gored by the tusks, shone forth even so.

**8.36.13**  Their limbs bathed in blood, they were like cataracts of red ochre, gleaming as mountains adorned with minerals stream down their slopes.

**8.36.14**  Many lances, hurled by the elephant-riders, that stood lodged and pointed back at them, those elephants whirled about with their trunks, and others broke them in pieces.

**8.36.15**  Their armor cleft by iron shafts, the noblest of elephants shone forth, O great king, like cloudless mountains at the coming of winter.

**8.36.16**  Studded with gold-feathered arrows, the noblest of elephants shone like mountains with their peaks ablaze with meteors, O honored sir.

**8.36.17**  Some elephants, huge as hills, struck down by other elephants, fell to the earth in that battle like winged mountains.

**8.36.18**  Other elephants ran wild, tormented by the barbs and afflicted by their wounds, and fell to the ground in that great battle upon their flanks and frontal-globes.

**8.36.19**  Others sank down like lions, bellowing fearful cries; and many drooped, O king, while still others trumpeted in pain.

**8.36.20**  And horses, slain by arrows, decked in golden trappings and housings, sank down and drooped and strayed off into the ten directions.

**8.36.21**  Others, dragged along and writhing upon the earth, struck by arrows and lances, took on many a contorted shape.

**8.36.22**  And men, struck down, lay groaning there upon the earth, O honored sir, some beholding their kinsmen, some their fathers, some their grandfathers.

**8.36.23**  And others, beholding their foes rushing at them there, O Bhārata, proclaimed to one another their renowned family names.

**8.36.24**  The severed arms of those men, O great king, adorned with gold, twisted up and twisted about, fell down and sprang up again.

**8.36.25**  They fell thus to the ground and quivered by the thousands, and others in the battle made violent leaps, twitching like serpents.

**8.36.26**  Those arms, like the coils of serpents, smeared with sandal-paste, O lord of peoples, drenched and red with blood, shone exceedingly bright, like golden standards.

**8.36.27**  As the dreadful melee thus raged on every side, men fought unrecognized, striking down one another.

**8.36.28**  With the field strewn with dust risen from the earth and crowded with the clash of weapons and wrapped in darkness, O king, neither friend nor foe could be told apart.

**8.36.29**  Such did that battle become, dreadful of aspect and terrifying; and great rivers whose waters were blood streamed forth there again and again.

**8.36.30**  Strewn with heads for boulders, with hair for moss and grass, choked with masses of bones, and bearing the finest of bows and arrows —

**8.36.31**  with flesh for mire and slough, those most terrible floods of blood set flowing rivers that swelled the realm of Yama.

**8.36.32**  Those rivers of dreadful aspect, bearing men to the abode of Yama, deep and engulfing, bred terror in the kṣatriyas as they plunged into them.

**8.36.33**  With carrion-eaters howling here and there, O tiger among men, the battlefield grew terrible, like unto the city of the king of the dead.

**8.36.34**  Countless headless trunks rose up on every side, and the hordes of spirits, sated with flesh and blood, danced indeed.

**8.36.35**  And having drunk the blood there and drunk the marrow-fat, O Bhārata, gorged on fat, marrow, and grease, and sated with flesh, the flocks of crows and vultures were seen rushing about.

**8.36.36**  But the heroes in the battle, O king, casting off fear so hard to cast off, renowned for their warrior's vow, did their deeds without dread.

**8.36.37**  On that field strewn with arrows and lances and thronged with bands of carrion-eaters, the heroes ranged about in companies, proclaiming their own valor.

**8.36.38**  To one another they made known their names and lineages, O Bhārata, and in the battle proclaimed all about their fathers' names and the names of their clans.

**8.36.39**  For many warriors there, O lord of peoples, calling out aloud, crushed one another with lances, javelins, and spears.

**8.36.40**  As that battle, dreadful of aspect and most terrible, raged on, the Kaurava army foundered, like a shattered ship upon the sea.

**8.37.1**  Saṃjaya said: As that battle raged then, and the kṣatriyas were going under, the mighty twang of the Gāṇḍīva was heard in the fight, O honored sir.

**8.37.2**  There the Pāṇḍava wrought havoc among the Saṃśaptakas, and among the Kosalas, O king, and among the Nārāyaṇa host.

**8.37.3**  But the Saṃśaptakas in the battle, greedy for victory and filled with fury, rained down a shower of arrows on every side upon the head of Pārtha.

**8.37.4**  That shower, O king, the mighty Pārtha, foremost of car-warriors, all at once stemmed with force and plunged into the battle, slaying as he went.

**8.37.5**  Having checked the array of chariots with heron-feathered shafts whetted on stone, Pārtha closed in battle upon Suśarman, that mighty car-warrior.

**8.37.6**  That best of car-warriors rained down showers of arrows upon him, and the saṃśaptakas too, standing firm in the fight, poured them upon Pārtha.

**8.37.7**  Then Suśarman, having pierced Pārtha with nine swift arrows, struck Janārdana on the right arm with three shafts, and with yet another broad-headed arrow he pierced the banner, O honoured one.

**8.37.8**  That great monkey upon the banner, O king, the work of Viśvakarman, gave a mighty roar and roared again, striking terror all around.

**8.37.9**  Hearing the cry of the monkey, your army, struck with terror and seized by overwhelming fear, became wholly motionless.

**8.37.10**  Then that host, standing motionless, gleamed, O king, like the grove of Caitraratha strewn with blossoms of every kind.

**8.37.11**  Then recovering their senses, O best of the Kurus, those warriors drenched Arjuna with arrows as rain-clouds drench a mountain, and they all encircled the great car of the Pāṇḍava.

**8.37.12**  Seizing forcibly and at once the horses, the chariot-wheels, and the chariot-shafts, O Bhārata, they then let loose a lion's roar.

**8.37.13**  Others seized the mighty arms of Keśava, while still others, O great king, joyfully laid hold of Pārtha as he stood upon his car.

**8.37.14**  But Keśava, shaking his arms there at the head of the battle, flung them all down as a rogue elephant flings other elephants.

**8.37.15**  Then Pārtha, enraged in battle, hemmed in by those great car-warriors, seeing his chariot held fast and Keśava set upon, struck down very many who rode in chariots and many footmen besides.

**8.37.16**  And then with close-fighting arrows he made the warriors who pressed close fall away in the fight, and he spoke thus to Keśava.

**8.37.17**  Behold, mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa, the bands of saṃśaptakas, who wrought a dreadful deed, now slain by me in their thousands.

**8.37.18**  There is no man upon earth, O bull of the Yadus, none in the world save myself, who could endure this dread blockade of the chariot.

**8.37.19**  Having spoken thus, Bībhatsu blew his Devadatta, and Kṛṣṇa too blew the Pāñcajanya, as though filling heaven and earth with sound.

**8.37.20**  Hearing that blast of the conches, the host of the saṃśaptakas reeled, O great king, and was sorely terrified.

**8.37.21**  Then the Pāṇḍava, slayer of hostile heroes, wrought a foot-binding snare, O great king, invoking again and again the serpent-weapon.

**8.37.22**  Those against whom Pārtha wrought the foot-binding snare in battle, bound by the foot-snare of the great-souled Pāṇḍava, became motionless, O king, as though made of iron.

**8.37.23**  Then the son of Pāṇḍu slew those motionless warriors, as Indra of old slew the Daityas in the battle at the killing of Tāraka.

**8.37.24**  Being slaughtered in the fight, they let go that splendid chariot and began to cast away all their weapons.

**8.37.25**  Then Suśarman, O lord of kings, seeing his host held captive, swiftly brought forth the Garuḍa-weapon, that great car-warrior.

**8.37.26**  Thereupon eagles swooped down, devouring the serpents, and those serpents fled away, O king, at the sight of the sky-rangers.

**8.37.27**  That host, freed from the foot-binding snare, shone forth, O lord of peoples, like the sun released from a mass of clouds, scorching the creatures.

**8.37.28**  Those warriors, set free, hurled volleys of arrows and volleys of weapons against the chariot of Phalguna, O honoured one.

**8.37.29**  Cutting apart that downpour wrought of mighty weapons with showers of his own arrows, the son of Vāsava, slayer of hostile heroes, then held the warriors at bay.

**8.37.30**  Then Suśarman, O king, having pierced Arjuna in the heart with a smooth-jointed arrow, struck him again with three more shafts; deeply wounded and pained, Arjuna sank down upon the floor of his chariot.

**8.37.31**  Then recovering his senses, he of the white horses and Kṛṣṇa for charioteer, that immeasurable one, swiftly brought forth the Aindra weapon; and thereupon thousands of arrows came into being, O honoured one.

**8.37.32**  In every quarter they were seen, destroying in battle elephants, horses, and chariots with their weapons by the hundreds of thousands, O king.

**8.37.33**  As the host was being slaughtered, a vast terror entered the bands of the saṃśaptakas and the cowherds, O Bhārata; for there was no man there who could fight back against Arjuna.

**8.37.34**  Before the very eyes of those heroes the great army was slain, and watching it being cut down, they stood helpless to exert their valour.

**8.37.35**  Having slain ten thousand warriors there in the fight, the son of Pāṇḍu blazed forth in battle, O king, like a smokeless fire flaming high.

**8.37.36**  Of those that remained, O Bhārata, there were fourteen thousand foot, ten thousand chariots, and three thousand tuskers.

**8.37.37**  Then the saṃśaptakas once more encircled Dhanaṃjaya, having resolved either to die or to win victory and turn him back.

**8.37.38**  And there a great battle was joined, O lord of peoples, between your men and the valiant, mighty Pāṇḍava, the diademed one.

**8.38.1**  Saṃjaya said: Kṛtavarman, Kṛpa, Droṇa's son, and the Sūta's son, O honoured one, Ulūka and the son of Subala, and the king together with his brothers—

**8.38.2**  seeing their army sinking, tormented by fear of the son of Pāṇḍu, sought eagerly to lift it up, as one would a foundering ship upon the sea.

**8.38.3**  Then for a moment, as it were, there was a most fierce battle, O Bhārata, breeding terror in the timid and swelling the joy of the brave.

**8.38.4**  The showers of arrows let loose by Kṛpa in the encounter the Sṛñjayas cut to pieces, like swarms of locusts.

**8.38.5**  Then Śikhaṇḍin, enraged, rushed swiftly upon the son of Gautama and rained showers of arrows on every side upon the Brāhmaṇa.

**8.38.6**  But Kṛpa, knower of mighty weapons, having beaten back that shower of arrows, in his wrath pierced Śikhaṇḍin in the fight with ten shafts.

**8.38.7**  Then Śikhaṇḍin, incensed, pierced Kṛpa most sorely in the battle with seven straight-flying, heron-feathered arrows.

**8.38.8**  Then Kṛpa, that great car-warrior, sorely pierced by sharp arrows, best of the twice-born, deprived the son of Pṛṣata of horses, charioteer, and car.

**8.38.9**  Then that great car-warrior, leaping down from the car whose horses were slain, seized shield and sword and rushed swiftly upon the Brāhmaṇa.

**8.38.10**  As he came rushing on, Kṛpa covered him at once in the fight with smooth-jointed arrows, and that seemed a wondrous thing.

**8.38.11**  There we beheld a marvel like stones floating upon water, that Śikhaṇḍin stood motionless in the fight, O king.

**8.38.12**  Seeing Śikhaṇḍin overwhelmed by Kṛpa, O best of kings, the great car-warrior Dhṛṣṭadyumna at once advanced against Kṛpa.

**8.38.13**  As Dhṛṣṭadyumna then advanced against the chariot of Śāradvata's son, the great car-warrior Kṛtavarman impetuously took him in hand.

**8.38.14**  And as Yudhiṣṭhira advanced against the chariot of Śāradvata's son, together with his sons and with his army, Droṇa's son held him off.

**8.38.15**  Nakula and Sahadeva, those two great car-warriors pressing forward, your son met and held back with a shower of arrows.

**8.38.16**  Bhīmasena, the Karūṣas, the Kekayas together with the Sṛñjayas—these Karṇa, son of Vikartana, held off in the battle, O Bhārata.

**8.38.17**  Then Kṛpa, son of Śaradvat, hastened to send arrows against Śikhaṇḍin in the fight, as though eager to consume him, O honoured one.

**8.38.18**  Those gold-adorned arrows sent by him on every side Śikhaṇḍin cut apart, swinging and whirling his sword again and again.

**8.38.19**  Then the son of Gautama swiftly shattered with his shafts the hundred-mooned shield of Pṛṣata's son, and thereupon the people raised a great cry.

**8.38.20**  Bereft of his shield, O great king, sword in hand, he rushed on, fallen into Kṛpa's power like a stricken man into the jaws of death.

**8.38.21**  Seeing that mighty warrior engulfed and harassed by the arrows of Śaradvat's son, Suketu, the son of Citraketu, O king, hastened forth.

**8.38.22**  Scattering many sharp arrows upon the Brāhmaṇa in the fight, that one of immeasurable spirit charged against the chariot of Gautama's son.

**8.38.23**  Seeing that Brāhmaṇa of steadfast vows irresistible in the fight, Śikhaṇḍin then swiftly withdrew, O best of kings.

**8.38.24**  Then Suketu, O king, having pierced the son of Gautama with nine arrows, struck him with seventy more, and again with three shafts.

**8.38.25**  Then he cut apart his bow together with the arrow set to it, O honoured one, and struck his charioteer sorely in a vital spot with a shaft.

**8.38.26**  But the son of Gautama, then enraged, taking up a new and firm bow, struck Suketu with thirty arrows in all his vital points.

**8.38.27**  With all his limbs unstrung, he reeled upon his splendid chariot, as a tree sways, violently shaken, in an earthquake.

**8.38.28**  From his swaying body Kṛpa, with a razor-edged arrow, struck down the head with its blazing earrings, its turban, and its helm.

**8.38.29**  That head fell upon the ground like a piece of flesh borne off by a hawk; then his body, falling thereafter, reached the earth.

**8.38.30**  When he was slain, O great king, his followers, struck with terror, abandoned the son of Gautama in the fight and fled to the ten quarters.

**8.38.31**  But the mighty Kṛtavarman, having checked Dhṛṣṭadyumna in the fight, said boldly to the son of Pṛṣata, "Stand, stand!"

**8.38.32**  Then there was a tumultuous battle between the Vṛṣṇi and the son of Pṛṣata in the field, like the strife of two hawks greedy for a morsel of flesh, O king.

**8.38.33**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna in the fight struck Hārdikya in the chest with nine arrows, in his wrath sorely afflicting the son of Hṛdika.

**8.38.34**  But Kṛtavarman, struck hard by the son of Pṛṣata in the fight, covered the son of Pṛṣata, chariot and horses, with his shafts.

**8.38.35**  Covered over with his chariot, O king, Dhṛṣṭadyumna could not be seen, like the sun veiled by clouds at the coming of the rains.

**8.38.36**  Shaking off that mass of arrows with his own gold-adorned shafts, Dhṛṣṭadyumna, though wounded, shone forth in battle, O king.

**8.38.37**  Then the son of Pṛṣata, enraged, that commander of the host, closing upon Kṛtavarman, let loose a most terrible shower of weapons.

**8.38.38**  That unbroken downpour of weapons rushing suddenly upon him Hārdikya scattered in the fight with many thousands of arrows.

**8.38.39**  Seeing his hard-to-withstand shower of weapons rent asunder in the fight, the son of Pṛṣata closed upon Kṛtavarman and held him in check.

**8.38.40**  And swiftly he sent his charioteer to the abode of Yama with a sharp-edged broad arrow; slain, the man fell from the chariot.

**8.38.41**  Then the mighty Dhṛṣṭadyumna, having vanquished that great car-warrior, his foe, at once held back the Kauravas in the fight with his shafts.

**8.38.42**  Thereupon your warriors rushed against Dhṛṣṭadyumna, and raising a lion's roar, the battle then went on.

**8.39.1**  Saṃjaya said: Droṇa's son, seeing Yudhiṣṭhira guarded by the grandson of Śini and by the valiant sons of Draupadī, advanced upon him as though in joy.

**8.39.2**  Scattering dread volleys of arrows, gold-nocked and whetted on stone, displaying his manifold passes of skill with a deft and nimble hand,

**8.39.3**  he then filled the sky with arrows charged by celestial-weapon mantras, and that master of arms hemmed in Yudhiṣṭhira in the fight.

**8.39.4**  Veiled by the arrows of Drauṇāyani, nothing whatever could be discerned; that whole front of the battlefield had become one mass of arrows.

**8.39.5**  That web of arrows poised in the sky, adorned with a meshwork of gold, shone forth, O best of the Bhāratas, like a great canopy stretched overhead.

**8.39.6**  With that gleaming net of arrows veiling the battle, O king, a shade as of clouds arose, the very sky barred over by shafts.

**8.39.7**  There we beheld a marvel, where such a thicket of arrows had formed: beholding the prowess of Droṇa's son, not one shaft fell to the ground.

**8.39.8**  Beholding the deftness of Droṇa's son, the great chariot-warriors were struck with wonder, O great king; and all those kings could not so much as look upon him, as upon the burning sun.

**8.39.9**  Though Sātyaki strove, and the King of Righteousness, the son of Pāṇḍu, and the other hosts as well, they could mount no prowess against him.

**8.39.10**  Then, as the host was being slaughtered, the great chariot-warriors who were Draupadī's sons, Sātyaki and the King of Righteousness, and the Pāñcālas too, all joined together; and casting off the dread fear of death, they rushed upon the son of Droṇa.

**8.39.11**  Sātyaki, having pierced Droṇa's son with twenty-five stone-tipped shafts, pierced him again with seven gold-adorned iron arrows.

**8.39.12**  Yudhiṣṭhira struck him with seventy-three, Prativindhya with seven, Śrutakarman with three arrows, and Śrutakīrti with seven.

**8.39.13**  Sutasoma with nine and Śatānīka with seven, and many other heroes pierced him on every side.

**8.39.14**  Then he, exceedingly wrathful, O king, hissing like a venomous serpent, pierced Sātyaki with twenty-five stone-whetted shafts.

**8.39.15**  Śrutakīrti with nine and Sutasoma with five, Śrutakarman with eight and Prativindhya with three arrows, Śatānīka with nine and the son of Dharma with seven.

**8.39.16**  Then he smote the other heroes with two shafts apiece, and with sharpened arrows he cut asunder the bow of Śrutakīrti.

**8.39.17**  Then, taking up another bow, Śrutakīrti the great chariot-warrior pierced Droṇa's son with three shafts and struck him again with other whetted arrows.

**8.39.18**  Then Droṇa's son, O great king, O Bhārata, with a torrent of arrows enveloped that host, and the kings on every side with his shafts.

**8.39.19**  Then again the immeasurable-souled son of Droṇa, laughing, cut asunder the bow of the King of Righteousness and pierced him with three arrows.

**8.39.20**  Then the son of Dharma, O king, taking up another mighty bow, pierced the son of Droṇa with seventy shafts and afflicted him in the arms and breast.

**8.39.21**  Then Sātyaki, enraged, with a sharp crescent-headed arrow cut the bow of Droṇa's son as he struck in the battle, and roared aloud.

**8.39.22**  His bow cut, Droṇa's son then, foremost of the mighty, with a spear swiftly struck down the charioteer of the Śaineya from his car.

**8.39.23**  Then, taking up another bow, the mighty son of Droṇa enveloped the Śaineya with a downpour of arrows, O Bhārata.

**8.39.24**  His horses, the chariot-driver fallen in the fray, bolted in flight, and were seen running this way and that, O Bhārata.

**8.39.25**  Then those warriors, with Yudhiṣṭhira at their head, rained upon the son of Droṇa, foremost of weapon-bearers, with force loosing their whetted shafts.

**8.39.26**  Beholding them advancing in fearful aspect, the scorcher of foes, the son of Droṇa, laughing, received them in the great battle.

**8.39.27**  Then the great chariot-warrior, son of Droṇa, blazing with a hundred shafts for his flames, burned up the thicket of the army in battle as fire burns dry brush in the forest.

**8.39.28**  That army of the son of Pāṇḍu, scorched by Droṇa's son, was thrown into tumult, O best of the Bhāratas, as a river's mouth is troubled by a great sea-monster.

**8.39.29**  And beholding, O great king, the prowess of Droṇa's son, they deemed all the Pāṇḍavas already slain by the son of Droṇa.

**8.39.30**  But Yudhiṣṭhira in haste closed with the son of Droṇa, that great chariot-warrior, and spoke to him, filled with wrath and indignation:

**8.39.31**  "You have, it seems, no affection, nor any gratitude, O tiger among men, that you seek this very day to slay me."

**8.39.32**  "By a brāhmaṇa austerity must be practiced, and giving, and study; by a kṣatriya the bow must be bent. You are a brāhmaṇa only in name."

**8.39.33**  "Before your very eyes, O mighty-armed one, I shall vanquish the Kauravas in battle. Do your deed in the fray; you are surely but a brāhmaṇa in name only."

**8.39.34**  Thus addressed, O great king, the son of Droṇa, smiling as it were, and reflecting on the justice of it, made no reply at all.

**8.39.35**  And saying nothing further, he covered the son of Pāṇḍu in battle with a torrent of arrows, wrathful as Death covering all creatures.

**8.39.36**  Being thus covered then by the son of Droṇa, O honored one, the Pārtha swiftly withdrew, abandoning his great host.

**8.39.37**  When that son of Dharma, Yudhiṣṭhira, had withdrawn, the son of Droṇa stood firm, O king, beyond reproach of the great-souled one.

**8.39.38**  Then King Yudhiṣṭhira, leaving the son of Droṇa in that great battle, set forth against your host, intent upon a cruel deed.

**8.40.1**  Saṃjaya said: Vaikartana himself, holding back Bhīmasena—accompanied by the Pāñcāla, hemmed about by the Cedis and the Kekayas—checked him with his shafts.

**8.40.2**  Then Karṇa, exceedingly enraged, slew the Cedis, the Karūṣas, and the Sṛñjaya great chariot-warriors before the very eyes of Bhīmasena.

**8.40.3**  Then Bhīmasena, leaving Karṇa, that best of chariot-warriors, advanced upon the Kaurava host, blazing like fire upon dry brushwood.

**8.40.4**  The son of the sūta too, in battle, slew the Pāñcālas and the Kekayas, and the great-bowmen Sṛñjayas, by the thousands.

**8.40.5**  Among the saṃśaptakas the Pārtha, among the Kauravas Vṛkodara, and among the Pāñcālas Karṇa—those great chariot-warriors wrought havoc.

**8.40.6**  Those warriors, consumed by those three who were like fire, went to destruction in the battle, O king, through your evil counsel.

**8.40.7**  Then Duryodhana, enraged, pierced Nakula with nine arrows, O best of the Bhāratas, and his four steeds as well.

**8.40.8**  Then again your son, the immeasurable-souled lord of men, with a razor-headed shaft cut down the golden standard of Sahadeva.

**8.40.9**  Then Nakula, enraged, struck your son with twenty-one shafts in the battle, O king, and Sahadeva with five.

**8.40.10**  Both those best of the Bhāratas, foremost of all bowmen, he in great wrath pierced in the breast, with five arrows and five.

**8.40.11**  Then with two more broad-headed arrows he cut through the bows of the twins, O king, laughing, and pierced them with seven.

**8.40.12**  Taking up two other bows, fair and excellent, resembling the bow of Indra, those two heroes shone in the fray, like the sons of the gods.

**8.40.13**  Then those two impetuous brothers, O king, rained dreadful arrows upon their brother in the battle, as two great clouds upon a mountain.

**8.40.14**  Then, enraged, O great king, your son, the great chariot-warrior, held back the two great-bowmen sons of Pāṇḍu with his feathered shafts.

**8.40.15**  His bow alone was seen as a circle in the battle, O Bhārata, and the arrows were seen issuing forth on every side.

**8.40.16**  Veiled by his shafts, the two sons of Pāṇḍu gleamed forth like the moon and the sun in the sky, cloud-shrouded, their radiance dimmed.

**8.40.17**  And those arrows, O great king, gold-feathered and stone-whetted, covered all the quarters then, like the rays of the sun.

**8.40.18**  When the sky had thus become a thicket of arrows and was veiled over, the form of the twins appeared like Yama, the all-ending Death.

**8.40.19**  Beholding that prowess of your son, the great chariot-warriors deemed the two sons of Mādrī to have come near to death.

**8.40.20**  Then the commander of the host of the great-souled Pāṇḍava, the son of Pṛṣata, O king, advanced thither, where King Suyodhana was.

**8.40.21**  Then, passing beyond the two heroic great chariot-warriors who were Mādrī's sons, Dhṛṣṭadyumna smote your son with his shafts.

**8.40.22**  Him the immeasurable-souled, exceedingly impatient son of yours pierced—the Pāñcāla prince—with twenty-five shafts, laughing, O bull among men.

**8.40.23**  Then again the immeasurable-souled son of yours, O lord of the earth, having pierced the Pāñcāla prince with sixty-five, roared aloud.

**8.40.24**  Then the king, with a keenly sharpened razor-edged arrow, cut his bow with the arrow set to it, and his hand-guard too, O honored one, in the battle.

**8.40.25**  Casting aside that severed bow, the Pāñcāla prince, harrower of foes, swiftly took up another, new and able to bear the strain.

**8.40.26**  Blazing as it were with impetuosity, his eyes red with fury, the great-bowman Dhṛṣṭadyumna, though wounded, shone resplendent.

**8.40.27**  He, Dhṛṣṭadyumna, seeking to slay that best of the Bhāratas, let fly fifteen iron arrows like hissing serpents.

**8.40.28**  Those stone-whetted shafts, fledged with heron and peacock feathers, piercing the king's gold-wrought armor, sank with force into the earth.

**8.40.29**  Sorely wounded, O great king, your son shone exceedingly, like a kiṃśuka tree in spring, dappled with blossoms, laden with flowers.

**8.40.30**  His armor cut by the iron arrows, his form battered by the blows, he in wrath cut asunder the bow of Dhṛṣṭadyumna with a broad-headed shaft.

**8.40.31**  Then, his bow cut, the lord of the earth in haste, O king, smote him with ten shafts between the brows.

**8.40.32**  Those shafts, burnished by the smith, adorned his face as bees greedy for nectar adorn a campaka in full bloom.

**8.40.33**  Casting aside that severed bow, the high-minded Dhṛṣṭadyumna swiftly took up another bow and sixteen broad-headed arrows.

**8.40.34**  Then, slaying Duryodhana's horses and his charioteer with five, he cut with a broad-headed shaft the gold-wrought bow.

**8.40.35**  The chariot with its gear, the parasol, the spear, the sword, the mace, and the standard—the son of Pṛṣata cut all of your son's with nine broad-headed arrows.

**8.40.36**  The bright standard of the Kuru lord, bearing the figure of an elephant wrought in gems and banded with refined gold, all the kings beheld cut down.

**8.40.37**  Duryodhana, chariotless, all his weapons cut away in the battle, his own brothers guarded round about, O bull of the Bhāratas.

**8.40.38**  Setting that lord of men upon his car, O king, Daṇḍadhāra in alarm bore him away, before the very eyes of Dhṛṣṭadyumna.

**8.40.39**  But Karṇa, the mighty one, coveting the king, having vanquished Sātyaki, advanced face to face in the battle against the fierce-arrowed slayer of Droṇa.

**8.40.40**  The Śaineya swiftly came up behind him, goading him with shafts, as one elephant gores another in the flank with its tusks.

**8.40.41**  There was, O Bhārata, a mighty battle, between Karṇa and the son of Pṛṣata, in the midst of your high-souled warriors.

**8.40.42**  Neither of the Pāṇḍavas nor of ours was any warrior seen turning his back, for that Karṇa had advanced in haste upon the Pāñcālas.

**8.40.43**  In that moment, O best of men, a destruction of elephants, steeds, and men arose on both sides, O king, when the day had reached its middle.

**8.40.44**  But the Pāñcālas, O great king, in haste and eager for victory, rushed upon Karṇa from every side, like birds upon a tree.

**8.40.45**  The son of Adhiratha, enraged, singling them out with the points of his arrows as they strove, those resolute ones, met them head-on in the front:

**8.40.46**  Vyāghraketu and Suśarman, and Śaṅku, and Ugra, and Dhanaṃjaya, and Śukla and Rocamāna, and Siṃhasena, hard to conquer—

**8.40.47**  Those heroes, with the speed of their chariots, encircled that best of men—the enraged Karṇa, splendid in battle, who was loosing his arrows.

**8.40.48**  But as those warriors fought on, the valiant lord of men, Rādheya, struck down eight of them with eight whetted arrows.

**8.40.49**  Then, O great king, the valiant son of the sūta, skilled in war, slew yet others—many thousands of warriors.

**8.40.50**  And in the battle, O king, he slew Viṣṇu and Viṣṇukarman, Devāpi and Bhadra, Daṇḍa, Citra, Citrāyudha, and Hari—

**8.40.51**  Siṃhaketu, Rocamāna, and Śalabha the great chariot-warrior; in fury he struck down even the great chariot-warriors of the Cedis.

**8.40.52**  As he took the lives of these, the form of Ādhirathi, his limbs drenched with blood, grew vast and mighty, like that of Rudra himself.

**8.40.53**  There, O Bhārata, the elephants struck by Karṇa's arrows, panic-stricken, fled in all directions, spreading great confusion.

**8.40.54**  Tormented by Karṇa's shafts, they fell to the earth in the battle, uttering manifold cries, like mountains shattered by the thunderbolt.

**8.40.55**  With elephants, horses, and men falling on every side, and with chariots overturned upon the road, the earth was strewn over.

**8.40.56**  Neither Bhīṣma nor Droṇa, nor any other of your men in war, performed such a deed as was done in that battle.

**8.40.57**  By the son of the sūta, O tiger among men, a great slaughter was wrought among elephants, chariots, horses, and men.

**8.40.58**  As a lion is seen ranging fearlessly amid the deer, so Karṇa moved without fear amid the Pāñcālas.

**8.40.59**  As a lion drives the terrified herds of deer to the four quarters, so Karṇa drove the throngs of Pāñcāla chariots.

**8.40.60**  As deer that come within the jaws of a lion can nowhere survive, so the great chariot-warriors who came upon Karṇa did not live.

**8.40.61**  As men are consumed when they come upon a blazing fire, so, O Bhārata, the Sṛñjayas were burned in battle by the fire that was Karṇa.

**8.40.62**  By Karṇa alone, O Bhārata, among the Cedis and the Pāñcālas, many esteemed as heroes were slain, each named aloud as he fell.

**8.40.63**  And to me, O lord of men, beholding Karṇa's prowess, it seemed that not a single Pāñcāla would escape alive from Ādhirathi in the battle.

**8.40.64**  Scattering the Pāñcālas in the fight, the valiant son of the sūta, in fury, rushed against Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Dharma.

**8.40.65**  Then Dhṛṣṭadyumna and the sons of Draupadī, O venerable one, and hundreds of other men surrounded that slayer of foes to protect the king.

**8.40.66**  Śikhaṇḍin and Sahadeva, Nakula and the son of Nakula, Janamejaya, the grandson of Śini, and many of the Prabhadrakas—

**8.40.67**  these, of boundless might, becoming the vanguard of Dhṛṣṭadyumna in the conflict, ranged against Karṇa as he loosed his missiles and arrows.

**8.40.68**  There in the fight Ādhirathi, alone, fell upon those many—Cedis, Pāñcālas, and Pāṇḍavas—as Garuḍa swoops upon serpents.

**8.40.69**  Bhīmasena, that mighty archer, enraged, battling alone in the fight against the Kurus, the Madras, and the Kekayas, shone resplendent.

**8.40.70**  There elephants struck by Bhīma's iron shafts in their vital parts, their riders slain, fell down and made the earth tremble.

**8.40.71**  Horses with their riders slain, and foot-soldiers bereft of life, lay pierced upon the field, vomiting blood in streams.

**8.40.72**  And chariot-warriors by the thousand, fallen with their weapons fallen from their hands, were seen lifeless—unwounded, slain by very terror of Bhīma.

**8.40.73**  With chariot-warriors, horses, charioteers, foot-soldiers, and elephants, all hewn apart by Bhīmasena's arrows, the earth was strewn over.

**8.40.74**  Tormented by Bhīmasena's might, the host of Duryodhana, O king, stood as though paralyzed—spiritless and grievously wounded.

**8.40.75**  Motionless and wretched in that great and tumultuous battle, it appeared, O king, like the ocean grown still with calm waters in its season.

**8.40.76**  That army of your son, endowed with wrath, valor, and strength, was then forcibly beaten down by arrows, and soaked in floods of blood, it grew drenched and dripping with gore.

**8.40.77**  The son of the sūta, enraged in battle, driving back the host of the Pāṇḍavas, and Bhīmasena driving back the Kurus, each shone in surpassing splendor.

**8.40.78**  As that fierce and wondrous battle went on, Arjuna, having slain in the midst of the host the many bands of the Saṃśaptakas—

**8.40.79**  —Arjuna, foremost of the victorious, then said to Vāsudeva: "This host that would fight on is broken, O Janārdana.

**8.40.80**  These great Saṃśaptaka chariot-warriors flee with all their bands, unable to withstand my arrows, as deer flee the lion's roar.

**8.40.81**  And the great host of the Sṛñjayas is being rent apart in this great battle, O Kṛṣṇa; yonder elephant-girth banner of the wise Karṇa is seen ranging again and again amid the royal army.

**8.40.82**  The other great chariot-warriors are not able to vanquish Karṇa in battle; for you know Karṇa to be a man of valor in prowess.

**8.40.83**  Drive there, then, to where Karṇa is putting our host to flight.

**8.40.84**  Or, avoiding the great chariot-warrior, the son of the sūta, drive on through the battle, that weariness may not oppress you, O Kṛṣṇa—or as it pleases you."

**8.40.85**  Hearing this, O great king, Govinda, as if smiling, said to Arjuna at once: "Swiftly slay the Kauravas, O son of Pāṇḍu."

**8.40.86**  Then the swan-white horses, urged on by Govinda, bearing Kṛṣṇa and the son of Pāṇḍu, entered into that great host of yours.

**8.40.87**  As the white horses adorned with gold, driven by Keśava, entered your army, it was rent asunder on all four sides.

**8.40.88**  Cleaving the great army and entering within, Keśava and Arjuna, enraged, their eyes red with fury, shone forth in mighty splendor.

**8.40.89**  Those two, eager for battle, summoned by their foes to the sacrifice of war, shone like the two Aśvin gods duly invoked by sacrificers at the rite.

**8.40.90**  Those two tigers among men, enraged, grew swift and impetuous, like two elephants goaded to fury by the slap of hands in the great battle.

**8.40.91**  Plunging into the array of chariots and the squadrons of horse, Phalguna ranged through the midst of the host like Death with noose in hand.

**8.40.92**  Seeing him advancing valiantly within your army in the fight, O Bhārata, your son once more urged on the bands of the Saṃśaptakas.

**8.40.93**  Then with a thousand chariots, three hundred elephants, and fourteen thousand horse in that great battle—

**8.40.94**  —and with two hundred thousand foot-soldiers and archers, heroes who had won renown and were known on every side, those great chariot-warriors advanced against the two heroes, overwhelming them.

**8.40.95**  Though covered over with arrows in the battle, that crusher of hostile hosts, the son of Pṛthā, revealing his terrible self like Death with noose in hand and slaying the Saṃśaptakas, became all the more a sight to behold.

**8.40.96**  Then, with arrows bright as lightning and adorned with gold, sped by the Diademed One, the very sky seemed without an interval between them.

**8.40.97**  With the great arrows loosed from the arm of the Diademed One falling thick, all things appeared overspread, O lord, as if by the brood of Kadrū.

**8.40.98**  Arrows golden-shafted, bright-pointed, and smooth-jointed, the Pāṇḍava of measureless soul displayed in all directions.

**8.40.99**  Having slain ten thousand of the Saṃśaptaka kings, the great chariot-warrior, the son of Kuntī, hastened against their flanking force.

**8.40.100**  Reaching that flanking force, guarded by the Kāmbojas, the son of Pṛthā crushed it by force with his arrows, as Vāsava crushed the Dānavas.

**8.40.101**  With his broad-headed shafts he swiftly cut away the weapon-grasping hands of the assailing foes, and their arms, and likewise their heads.

**8.40.102**  With their limbs and members severed, weaponless, they fell to the earth, like many-branched trees shattered by a wind blowing on every side.

**8.40.103**  Upon Arjuna, as he slew the throngs of elephants, horses, chariots, and foot, the younger brother of Sudakṣiṇa rained down a shower of arrows.

**8.40.104**  As he shot, Arjuna struck off with two crescent-headed arrows his two arms, like iron bludgeons, and with a razor-edged shaft his head, whose face was bright as the full moon.

**8.40.105**  He fell then from his mount, streaming with his own blood, like a peak of a realgar-red mountain riven by the thunderbolt.

**8.40.106**  They beheld the younger brother of Sudakṣiṇa, the Kāmboja, slain—tall, his eyes like lotus-petals, exceedingly fair to look upon, resembling a pillar of gold, like a golden mountain cleft asunder.

**8.40.107**  Then once more the battle grew terrible and wondrous to behold, and many were the conditions of the warriors fighting there.

**8.40.108**  With the horses of these Kāmbojas, Yavanas, and Śakas struck down and smeared with blood, all then became red, O lord of peoples.

**8.40.109**  By chariots, by chariot-horses and charioteers, by horses with their riders slain, by elephants with their riders slain, and by mahouts whose elephants were slain, O great king, a fearful destruction of men was wrought upon one another.

**8.40.110**  As that flanking force and the wing were being slain by the great-souled one, the son of Droṇa hastened against Arjuna, foremost of the victorious.

**8.40.111**  Brandishing his great bow adorned with gold, and taking up his terrible arrows like the sun gathering its own rays.

**8.40.112**  By those arrows loosed by the son of Droṇa, falling on every side, O great king, the two—Kṛṣṇa and Dhanaṃjaya—standing in their chariot, were covered over.

**8.40.113**  Then with hundreds of sharp arrows the valiant son of Bhāradvāja rendered both Mādhava and the son of Pāṇḍu motionless in the battle.

**8.40.114**  All things, moving and unmoving, broke into cries of woe, seeing the two protectors of all that moves and is still covered over with arrows.

**8.40.115**  And the hosts of Siddhas and Cāraṇas gathered together from every side, anxiously wondering whether the welfare of the worlds might yet be preserved this day.

**8.40.116**  Never before, O king, had I beheld such prowess as arose in the son of Droṇa as he covered the two Kṛṣṇas over with arrows.

**8.40.117**  The twang of the son of Droṇa's bow, terrifying his foes in the battle, I heard again and again, O king, like the roaring of a lion.

**8.40.118**  And his bowstring, as he ranged through the battle shooting to left and right, became like lightning flashing in the midst of the clouds.

**8.40.119**  Then the Pāṇḍava, swift of action and firm of hand though he was, fell into utter bewilderment and gazed upon the son of Droṇa.

**8.40.120**  He deemed his own prowess robbed from him by that exceedingly great-souled one; and his form in the battle, O king, became hard to look upon.

**8.40.121**  As the great battle between the son of Droṇa and the Pāṇḍava went on thus, O lord of kings, with the mighty son of Droṇa waxing strong and the son of Kuntī failing, wrath came upon Kṛṣṇa.

**8.40.122**  Sighing in his anger, O king, and seeming to burn with his very eyes, he looked again and again upon the son of Droṇa and upon Phalguna in the battle.

**8.40.123**  Then in his wrath Kṛṣṇa spoke to the son of Pṛthā with affection: "Most wondrous is this I see of you in battle, O Pārtha—that the son of Droṇa surpasses you this day, O Bhārata.

**8.40.124**  Is the Gāṇḍīva yet in your hand, and do you stand upon your chariot, O Arjuna? Are your two arms unharmed? Is your valor still the same as ever?"

**8.40.125**  Thus addressed by Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna, hastening in that hour for haste, loosed fourteen broad-headed shafts and cut asunder the bow of the son of Droṇa, and his standard, parasol, and banner, his chariot, his lance, and his mace.

**8.40.126**  And he smote him hard upon the collarbone with calf-tooth arrows; and the son of Droṇa, falling into a deep swoon, sank down clinging to his banner-staff.

**8.40.127**  That senseless man, O great king, tormented by fear of the Diademed One, his charioteer bore away from the battle, guarding him from Dhanaṃjaya.

**8.40.128**  At that very moment, Vijaya, the scorcher of foes, slew your army by the hundreds and by the thousands, even as that heroic son of yours looked on, O Bhārata.

**8.40.129**  Thus did this slaughter of your men come to pass against the foe — cruel, ruinous, and dreadful, O king, born of your evil counsel.

**8.40.130**  The son of Kuntī crushed the saṃśaptakas, and Vṛkodara the Kurus, and the Pāñcāla scattered Vasuṣeṇa utterly in the fray.

**8.41.1**  Saṃjaya said: Kṛṣṇa, hastening, again spoke softly to Pārtha: "Behold, O Kauravya, the king, and the Pāṇḍavas withdrawing."

**8.41.2**  "Behold Karṇa in the great arena, blazing like fire; and yonder the dread mighty archer Bhīma has turned back to the battle."

**8.41.3**  "After him these turn back, led by Dhṛṣṭadyumna — the foremost of the Pāñcālas, the Sṛñjayas, and the Pāṇḍavas; and by the returning Pārthas the great host of the enemy is broken."

**8.41.4**  "This Karṇa, O Arjuna, stays the fleeing Kauravas — like Death himself in his onset, equal to Śakra in valor."

**8.41.5**  "Yonder goes Drauṇi, O Kauravya, the best of those who bear weapons; and Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the great chariot-warrior, rushes upon him in the fray."

**8.41.6**  All this the unassailable Vāsudeva recounted to the Diademed One; and thereupon, O king, a most terrible, mighty battle arose.

**8.41.7**  And lion-roars rang out there at the clash, O king, of both the armies — who had made of death their only turning-back.

**8.42.1**  Saṃjaya said: Then the Kurus and the Sṛñjayas, unafraid, came together once more — the Pārthas with Yudhiṣṭhira at their head, and we with Vaikartana at ours.

**8.42.2**  Then there began a fearful, hair-raising battle between Karṇa and the Pāṇḍavas, swelling the realm of Yama.

**8.42.3**  When that tumultuous battle, whose waters were blood, had broken forth — the valiant saṃśaptakas being now but a remnant, O Bhārata —

**8.42.4**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O great king, together with all the kings, and the Pāṇḍava great chariot-warriors, charged straight at Karṇa.

**8.42.5**  As they came on in the fray, exultant and eager for victory, Karṇa alone withstood them in the battle, as a mountain withstands floods of water.

**8.42.6**  Reaching that Karṇa, those great chariot-warriors were scattered, as floods of water are scattered in all directions on meeting a mountain; and between the two, O great king, the battle was hair-raising.

**8.42.7**  But Dhṛṣṭadyumna, in his wrath, struck Rādheya with a smooth-jointed shaft and cried, "Stand! Stand!"

**8.42.8**  But the great chariot-warrior, brandishing Vijaya, his peerless bow, cut asunder the bow of Pārṣata and, full of wrath, smote the son of Pṛṣata with nine shafts like venomous serpents.

**8.42.9**  Those shafts, having pierced the gold-wrought armor of that great-souled one, gleamed smeared with blood like śakra-gopa beetles, O sinless one.

**8.42.10**  Casting aside that severed bow, the great chariot-warrior Dhṛṣṭadyumna took up another bow, and with arrows like venomous serpents pierced Karṇa with seventy smooth-jointed shafts.

**8.42.11**  In like manner, O king, Karṇa too, that mighty archer, pierced with sharpened shafts the scorcher of foes, Pārṣata, the enemy of Droṇa.

**8.42.12**  At him Karṇa, O great king, in his fury sped a gold-adorned shaft, like a second rod of Death.

**8.42.13**  As it swiftly fell upon him, that shaft of dreadful form, O lord of peoples, the grandson of Śini, deft of hand, cut it sevenfold, O king.

**8.42.14**  Seeing his arrow thus brought down by shafts, O lord of peoples, Karṇa enveloped Sātyaki on every side with a shower of arrows.

**8.42.15**  And he pierced him in that battle with seven iron shafts; the grandson of Śini in turn pierced him with arrows adorned with gold.

**8.42.16**  Then the combat grew exceedingly fierce, terrifying to eye and ear, O king — dreadful and wondrous, and on every side worth the beholding.

**8.42.17**  In all the beings there a thrill of horror arose, beholding that feat in battle of Karṇa and the grandson of Śini, O king.

**8.42.18**  In this interval Drauṇi advanced against the exceedingly mighty Pārṣata, tamer of foes, destroyer of the valor and the lives of enemies.

**8.42.19**  Dhanaṃjaya being far off, Drauṇi spoke in his wrath: "Stand! Stand this day, slayer of a Brāhmaṇa — you shall not escape me alive!"

**8.42.20**  So saying, the swift-handed hero, that great chariot-warrior, straining to his utmost might, overwhelmed with sharp, keen-edged, dreadful shafts the Pārṣata, who likewise strove with all his power.

**8.42.21**  For as Drauṇi, beholding Pārṣata in battle, O honored one, so too the Pārṣata, slayer of hostile heroes, beholding Drauṇi in the fray, grew not greatly glad of heart, but deemed his own death at hand.

**8.42.22**  But Drauṇi, O lord of kings, seeing Dhṛṣṭadyumna standing firm in the fray, hissing with rage, the hero rushed upon the Pārṣata; and at the mere sight of each other the two were seized with utmost fury.

**8.42.23**  Then the valiant son of Droṇa, O great king, said in haste to Dhṛṣṭadyumna who stood near, O lord of peoples: "Basest of the Pāñcālas, this day I shall send you to your death!"

**8.42.24**  "The sinful deed you wrought of old in slaying Droṇa shall this day fall back upon you, as surely as ill-doing brings its fruit."

**8.42.25**  "If, unguarded by Pārtha, you stand your ground in the fight, and do not flee, O fool — this truth I tell you."

**8.42.26**  Thus addressed, the valiant Dhṛṣṭadyumna answered in reply: "That same sword of mine shall give you what it gave your father as he strove in the fight."

**8.42.27**  "If indeed Droṇa, that so-called Brāhmaṇa, was slain by me, how then shall I not now slay you in battle by my valor?"

**8.42.28**  So saying, O great king, the wrathful commander, the Pārṣata, pierced Drauṇi with a sharpened arrow.

**8.42.29**  Then Drauṇi, exceedingly enraged, with smooth-jointed shafts veiled all the quarters about Dhṛṣṭadyumna in the fight, O king.

**8.42.30**  Neither the sky, nor the quarters, nor the warriors round about could be seen, O great king, so veiled were they by arrows in their thousands.

**8.42.31**  In like manner the Pārṣata, O king, veiled with arrows Drauṇi, who shone in the fray, even as the son of the sūta looked on.

**8.42.32**  Rādheya too, O great king, alone held in check the Pāñcālas together with the Pāṇḍavas, the sons of Draupadī, Yudhāmanyu, and the great chariot-warrior Sātyaki — a sight to behold on every side.

**8.42.33**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna too in the fray cut asunder Drauṇi's bow; casting aside that severed bow, Drauṇi took up another, swift and dreadful in battle, with arrows like venomous serpents.

**8.42.34**  He, O lord of kings, in the twinkling of an eye shattered with his shafts the Pārṣata's bow, his lance, his mace, his banner, his horses, his charioteer, and his chariot itself.

**8.42.35**  He, his bow cut, his chariot lost, his horses slain, his charioteer slain, took up a broad sword and a hundred-mooned shield, gleaming bright.

**8.42.36**  But Drauṇi too, O lord of kings, that great chariot-warrior, swift of hand and firm in arms, the hero swiftly cut these asunder in the fray with broad-headed shafts — and that, while he had not stepped down from his chariot, seemed a marvel.

**8.42.37**  Yet Dhṛṣṭadyumna — chariotless, his horses slain, his bow cut, pierced again and again by shafts and hewn to pieces by weapons — that great chariot-warrior, for all his striving, the foremost of the Bhāratas could not overcome.

**8.42.38**  When Drauṇi could not make an end of him with his arrows, O king, then the hero, casting aside his bow, hastily pursued the Pārṣata.

**8.42.39**  The onset of that great-souled one as he rushed forth, O king, was like that of Garuḍa swooping down, eager to seize the foremost of serpents.

**8.42.40**  At that very moment Mādhava said to Arjuna: "Behold, O Pārtha, how Drauṇi makes mighty effort to slay the Pārṣata — without doubt he will slay him."

**8.42.41**  "Deliver him, O mighty-armed — the Pārṣata, scorcher of foes, who has come into the jaws of Drauṇi as one fallen into the jaws of Death."

**8.42.42**  So saying, O great king, the valiant Vāsudeva sped the horses thither, to where Drauṇi was stationed.

**8.42.43**  Those horses, bright as the moon, urged on by Keśava, sped toward Drauṇi's chariot as though drinking up the sky.

**8.42.44**  Seeing the two of mighty valor, Kṛṣṇa and Dhanaṃjaya, coming on, the strong one, O king, redoubled his effort to slay Dhṛṣṭadyumna.

**8.42.45**  At the mere sight of Dhṛṣṭadyumna being dragged off, O lord of men, the mighty Pārtha hurled his shafts against Drauṇi.

**8.42.46**  Those gold-wrought arrows, sped forcefully from the Gāṇḍīva, reaching Drauṇi entered him as serpents enter an anthill.

**8.42.47**  Scattered by those dreadful shafts, the valiant son of Droṇa, tormented by the arrows of Dhanaṃjaya, the hero mounted his chariot, and seizing his peerless bow pierced Pārtha with his darts.

**8.42.48**  In this interval the hero Sahadeva, O ruler of men, bore the Pārṣata, scorcher of foes, away from the fray upon his chariot.

**8.42.49**  Arjuna too, O great king, pierced Drauṇi with feathered shafts; and the son of Droṇa, enraged, smote him upon the arms and the breast.

**8.42.50**  But Pārtha, enraged in battle, hurled at the son of Droṇa an iron shaft, the measure of Doom, like a second rod of Time; and it, of great splendor, fell upon the Brāhmaṇa's shoulder.

**8.42.51**  Reeling, O great king, from the force of that shaft in the fight, he sank down upon the floor of his chariot and fell into utter distress.

**8.42.52**  Then Karṇa, O great king, drew the bow named Vijaya, gazing again and again in wrath upon Arjuna in the fray, longing for single combat with Pārtha in that great battle.

**8.42.53**  But the charioteer, leaving aside that stricken hero, harrier of foes, bore him in haste away from the field of battle upon his chariot.

**8.42.54**  Then a great shout went up, O great king, from the Pāñcālas flushed with victory, beholding the Pārṣata delivered and the son of Droṇa hard pressed.

**8.42.55**  And divine instruments sounded forth by the thousands, and a lion-roar arose at the sight of that dreadful, great marvel.

**8.42.56**  Having done thus, Pārtha Dhanaṃjaya said to Vāsudeva: "Drive on to the saṃśaptakas, O Kṛṣṇa — this is my supreme task."

**8.42.57**  Then the Dāśārha, hearing the Pāṇḍava's word, drove onward in his richly bannered chariot, swift as the mind and the wind.

**8.43.1**  Saṃjaya said: In this interval Kṛṣṇa spoke a word to Pārtha, as though pointing out to the son of Kuntī the Dharma-king Yudhiṣṭhira.

**8.43.2**  "Yonder, O Pāṇḍava, your brother is swiftly pursued by the mighty sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, great archers bent on his slaying, O Pārtha."

**8.43.3**  "And the Pāñcālas, enraged and frenzied in battle, follow after at great speed, seeking to save the great-souled Yudhiṣṭhira."

**8.43.4**  "Yonder Duryodhana, O Pārtha, mailed and ringed by his host of chariots — that king of all the world — runs in pursuit of the king."

**8.43.5**  "Eager to slay that tiger among men, the mighty one, together with his brothers — whose touch is like a venomous serpent's, skilled in every mode of war."

**8.43.6**  "These sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra advance with their elephants, horses, chariots, and foot, eager to seize Yudhiṣṭhira as suitors covet a most precious jewel."

**8.43.7**  "Behold — the lord, hemmed in and guarded by the Sātvata and by Bhīma, as the Daityas, eager to carry off the nectar, were held helpless by Śakra and Agni."

**8.43.8**  "By force of their numbers these great chariot-warriors, hastening, advance again upon the Pāṇḍava, as floods of water flow to the sea in the rainy season."

**8.43.9**  "Roaring with lion-roars, blowing too their conch-shells, those strong mighty archers, brandishing their bows withal —"

**8.43.10**  "I deem the son of Kuntī, Yudhiṣṭhira, fallen into the jaws of Death, offered up into the fire — blessings on you — gone into Duryodhana's power."

**8.43.11**  "For so well arrayed is the host of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son, O Pāṇḍava, that not even Śakra, once come within range of its arrows, could win free of it."

**8.43.12**  "The arrow-force of the valiant Duryodhana, of Drauṇi, of the son of Śaradvat, and of Karṇa, could rend asunder even the mountains."

**8.43.13**  When valiant Duryodhana hurls his floods of arrows swiftly, like Death himself enraged, who in battle could withstand his onslaught?

**8.43.14**  And by Karṇa, that scorcher of foes—strong, swift of hand, accomplished, and skilled in war—the king has been turned back.

**8.43.15**  Rādheya, that hero, joined with the high-souled sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, is able in battle to press hard even the foremost of the Pāṇḍavas.

**8.43.16**  As Pārtha fought thus in the engagement with self-possessed soul, his armor was stripped away even by other great chariot-warriors.

**8.43.17**  Wasted by fasting, O best of the Bhāratas, the king stands firm in the strength of the brahman, not, O lord, in the overwhelming strength of the warrior.

**8.43.18**  I think, O Pārtha, that the great king Yudhiṣṭhira no longer lives, since the wrathful Bhīmasena now endures these lion-roars.

**8.43.19**  of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O subduer of foes, who again and again roar and blow their great conches in the battle, flushed with the look of conquest—

**8.43.20**  "Yudhiṣṭhira the Pāṇḍava is slain!"—so, O bull of the Bhāratas, does Karṇa urge on the mighty sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

**8.43.21**  Veiling the king with the Sthūṇākarṇa, the Indrajāla, and the Pāśupata weapons, O Pārtha, the great chariot-warriors pursue him. I deem the king to be in distress, O Bhārata, and in need of being attended to.

**8.43.22**  For the Pāñcālas with the Pāṇḍavas, foremost of all weapon-bearers, follow after him in haste at this urgent hour, eager to lift him up though he sinks as it were into the abyss, mighty though he is.

**8.43.23**  The king's banner is no longer seen, cut down by Karṇa's arrows, O Pārtha, before the very eyes of the twins, of Sātyaki, and of Śikhaṇḍin—

**8.43.24**  of Dhṛṣṭadyumna, of Bhīma, or of Śatānīka, O lord, and of all the Pāñcālas and the Cedis as well, O Bhārata.

**8.43.25**  There Karṇa in battle, O Pārtha, shatters the Pāṇḍavas' host with his arrows, as an elephant tramples a bed of lotuses.

**8.43.26**  Your chariot-warriors are fleeing, O joy of Pāṇḍu; look, look, O Pārtha, how these great chariot-warriors give way!

**8.43.27**  These elephants, O Bhārata, struck down by Karṇa in the battle, uttering cries of anguish, scatter to the ten directions.

**8.43.28**  Behold on every side, O Pārtha, the host of chariots in flight, driven before him in the battle by Karṇa, that grinder of foes.

**8.43.29**  Behold in the battle the elephant-girth pennon ranging here and there, the banner of the Sūta's son who stands upon his car, O best of those who bear banners.

**8.43.30**  Yonder Rādheya rushes toward Bhīmasena's chariot, scattering as it were hundreds of arrows and smiting your host.

**8.43.31**  Behold these Pāñcālas, driven and slain by that great-souled one in the mighty fray, as the Daityas were by Śakra.

**8.43.32**  Having vanquished in battle the Pāñcālas, the Pāṇḍavas, and the Sṛñjayas, Karṇa looks in every direction—seeking you, I think.

**8.43.33**  Behold, O Pārtha, how fair he shines as he draws his peerless bow, like Śakra, having conquered his foes, surrounded by the throngs of gods.

**8.43.34**  These Kauravas shout aloud, beholding Karṇa's prowess, terrifying in the battle the Pārthas and the Sṛñjayas by the thousands.

**8.43.35**  Having with all his might struck terror into the Pāṇḍavas in the great battle, Rādheya, the honored one, addresses all the troops:

**8.43.36**  "Charge, advance, run swiftly, O Kauravas, so that no Sṛñjaya may escape from you alive in the fight!"

**8.43.37**  "Do this, girt for the work; we shall follow at your back." Having spoken thus, he advanced behind them, scattering arrows.

**8.43.38**  Behold Karṇa in the battle, O Pārtha, radiant with his white complexion, like the day-maker sun lighting up the mountain of dawn.

**8.43.39**  and with the parasol borne above his head in the battle, O Bhārata, white as the full moon and ribbed with a hundred staves.

**8.43.40**  Karṇa watches for you with sidelong glance, O lord of peoples; summoning his utmost effort, he will surely come against you in the encounter.

**8.43.41**  Behold him, O mighty-armed one, brandishing his great bow and loosing arrows like venomous serpents, that man of vast strength.

**8.43.42**  Yonder Rādheya is seen returning, O you of the monkey banner, and he comes on to his own destruction, like a moth toward a flame.

**8.43.43**  Seeing Karṇa alone, O Bhārata, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, well girt and seeking to protect him, advances with his array of chariots.

**8.43.44**  That evil-souled one, together with all these, must be slain with effort by you who desire glory and kingdom and the highest happiness.

**8.43.45**  Considering yourself, your soul made perfect, O bull of the Bhāratas, and Rādheya, who has done wrong against the righteous Yudhiṣṭhira—

**8.43.46**  set yourself against Rādheya, for the time has come at once; fixing your mind on the noble resolve in war, advance against that leader of chariot-bands.

**8.43.47**  For five hundred of these chief chariots, O best of charioteers, come on with speed, manned by the strong and dreadful in splendor.

**8.43.48**  Five thousand elephants, twice as many horses, O son of Kuntī, and footmen by the myriad, all banded together—that host, each guarding the other, O hero, moves against you.

**8.43.49**  Against the Sūta's son, that great archer, show yourself by your own might; summoning your utmost effort, O bull of the Bhāratas, go forth to meet him.

**8.43.50**  Yonder Karṇa, in fury, rushes upon the Pāñcālas; for I see his banner moving toward Dhṛṣṭadyumna's chariot. He will utterly destroy the Pāñcālas, I think, O scorcher of foes.

**8.43.51**  I tell you glad tidings, O Pārtha, and thus it is, O bull of the Bhāratas: the king lives, the Kaurava Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Dharma!

**8.43.52**  Yonder the mighty-armed Bhīma has turned back at the head of the host, surrounded by the Sṛñjaya troops and by Sātyaki, O Bhārata.

**8.43.53**  These Kauravas are being slain in the battle by sharp arrows, O son of Kuntī, by Bhīmasena and by the high-souled Pāñcālas.

**8.43.54**  For the army of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son has turned its face from the battle and flees in haste, smitten by Bhīma's arrows.

**8.43.55**  Like a field whose crop has failed, drenched with blood, the Bhārata army, O best of the Bhāratas, is a piteous sight to behold.

**8.43.56**  Behold, O son of Kuntī, Bhīmasena, that lord of warriors, returned like a furious venomous serpent; before him the host takes flight.

**8.43.57**  Banners yellow, red, black, and white, adorned with stars, moon, and sun, are scattered abroad, and these parasols too, O Arjuna.

**8.43.58**  Standards of gold and silver and of bright metal, of many kinds, are cast down, and the elephants and horses are scattered.

**8.43.59**  These chariot-warriors fall from their cars, their lives fled, slain by the many-colored arrows of the unyielding Pāñcālas.

**8.43.60**  The swift Pāñcālas charge upon the masterless elephants, horses, and chariots, and upon the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Dhanaṃjaya.

**8.43.61**  And those tigers among men, relying on Bhīmasena, crush the unassailable strength of the foe, recking nothing of their lives, O subduer of enemies.

**8.43.62**  These Pāñcālas roar and blow their conches, and they charge in battle, smiting their foes with arrows.

**8.43.63**  Behold the glory of heaven, O scorcher of foes: the Pāñcālas, enraged, smite down the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra as lions slay elephants.

**8.43.64**  On every side this great host of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son is overwhelmed by the Pāñcālas, as the Gaṅgā is by swift swans coming from Lake Mānasa.

**8.43.65**  Mightily do the heroes Kṛpa, Karṇa, and the rest exert themselves to check the Pāñcālas, like bulls among bulls.

**8.43.66**  The heroes led by Dhṛṣṭadyumna slay by the thousand those great chariot-warriors of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son, deeply sunk under Bhīma's weapons; and the great host of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son has its chariots for the most part downcast.

**8.43.67**  Behold these elephants, cleft by Bhīma's iron shafts, falling like the peaks of mountains struck by the thunderbolt of the wielder of the bolt.

**8.43.68**  Pierced by the well-jointed arrows of Bhīmasena, these great elephants flee, trampling their own ranks.

**8.43.69**  Do you not recognize, O Arjuna, the unbearable lion-roar of Bhīma, the hero flushed with conquest, as he shouts in the battle?

**8.43.70**  There the Niṣāda's son comes upon the Pāṇḍava with his lordly elephant, raging to slay him with lances, like Death with rod in hand.

**8.43.71**  His two arms, lances and all, were severed by the roaring Bhīma, and he was struck down by ten keen iron shafts bright as tongues of fire.
